Prune list declared NON-exhaustive (unlisted derived files over-scan, never under-scan). The stamp is registered on every guard surface (Write/Edit deny tuple + message, both Bash PROTECTED regexes, `.gitignore`, `paths.md`).
- **Dual blind round-1, all folded pre-ship.** Execution reviewer (7 attack lanes): SHIP — batch isolation held under PATH-strip mid-batch, dedupe/whitespace arg abuse, legacy layouts, monorepo PREFIX, exit-8 protected-path bleed in both orderings, deny live-fire (`tee`/`sed -i`/`rm+touch` all denied), and the widened Stop prune provably did NOT eat the evidence class (shell-written `postflight.json` still re-triggers). Static reviewer: FIX-FIRST, 9 findings folded — the exit-2 whole-gate-skip pair, the rotted (and unenforced) `build-dispatch-prompt.sh` anchor-regex pointer (now line-number-free AND pinned byte-identical by test), the near-Critical baseline-timing shift (step-1 re-capture restored), a vacuous mutual-prune test arm (now forces a genuine scan), batch-wide provenance stamps (now per-unit), the deny message not naming what it blocks, four unpruned derived files, and two untested legacy probe arms (both now fixtured). Carried open, disclosed at the guard: depth-1 deletions under a probe root and backdated-mtime shell edits evade `find -newer` — stale side is an advisory state file only, same class as shipped 4c.
- Pinned by `tests/token-efficiency/test-4de-batch-preflight-ptu-debounce.sh` (batch parity vs single, fail-fast + remainder disclosure, exit-7 collection + idempotent re-batch, skip/rescan on every input class, live mutual-prune both directions, legacy-tree probes, anchor-regex parity, no-mint, guard-surface census). `tests/hooks/bounded-subprocess.test.sh`'s halt probe extended (hardened, not loosened): a `die()` helper counts as a halt ONLY when its body provably `sys.exit(code)`s — both directions controlled. Skill: execute-bolts 2.28.0 → 2.29.0.

## [5.22.0] - 2026-08-01 — tranche 4 (first release, 4a+4b+4c): the hooks stopped paying spawn tax on idle turns

Phase 4 of [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md) ships in TWO releases by design — 4a/4b/4c here (the two HIGHs + the largest MED, all hook-side); 4d/4e/4f deferred with rationale in the spec (4f touches the B1 recompute, the riskiest moat surface in the set — it gets its own round). Zero-token changes confined to hooks/scripts the model never reads; Windows/CrowdStrike (~220 ms/spawn) is where the minutes live.

- **4a-i — the framework-pack resolver gained a derived cache with a ZERO-EXEC hit path.** `_lib/resolve-framework-pack.sh` is called by 4 gate-firing validators per execute-bolts PreToolUse firing (the audit's measured 9 spawns/firing); the answer changes only when two project files or the pack files change. The cache (`.mega-sdd/.cache/pack-resolver/`, one file per section/chain request storing the cold run's EXACT stdout + rc) validates with bash BUILTINS only — `[ -f ]` existence parity + `[ cache -nt input ]` over both project inputs, every pack file in the cached chain, and the pack-root dir — zero child processes on a hit, stdout byte-identical (proven by a PATH-shim spy: zero pythons). Equal-second writes are a documented SAFE miss (a wasted resolve, never a stale answer — do not "fix" by accepting equal mtimes). The cache is derived and discardable, never state.
- **4a-ii — the five `validate-bolt-artifacts.sh` calls became ONE composable invocation** at both call sites (the PreToolUse execute-bolts gate block, with `--recompute`; the Stop hook, without — the read-only lane unchanged). The identical per-block guards hoisted, each scan records its rc, a multi-scan run exits with the MAX rc; every scan's python, state file, and verdict semantics untouched — call-site consolidation, never a scan merge (the research's deeper 40→8 python-merge ceiling deliberately out of scope). Proven on a real orphan-FAIL fixture: multi rc == singles max, same five state files from one call; the whole bolt-orphans/batch-suite-gate/postflight-evidence suites pass unchanged.
- **4c — the Stop hook's five artifact scans now run only on turns that could change their verdict.** Old guard: `[ -d .mega-sdd ]` — ~40 execs (20 git) every turn end, the audit's ~24.9 min per 400-tool-call run. New: a `.mega-sdd/.stop-scan-stamp` records HEAD at the last scan; the scans re-run when HEAD moved, when anything under `.mega-sdd/` (or legacy `docs/mega-sdd/`) changed since the stamp (`find -newer`, memory/+.cache pruned — the hook's own debug-log append can never defeat the skip), when the stamp is absent, or when HEAD is unresolvable — fail TOWARD scanning, stamp written only after the scans complete. A skipped turn leaves still-true verdicts, and the PreToolUse gate re-derives all five states anyway (S6 EB-GATE-1), so a stale skip can never open a gate. Idle-turn cost: ~3 probe execs instead of 5 validator spawn-chains.
- **4b — always-paid plumbing on the three hot hooks became builtins** (bash-3.2-safe): stdin `cat` → `IFS= read -r -d ''`; `$(cd $(dirname $0) && pwd)` → `${0%/*}` with an absolute-path guard + subshell fallback; the pre-tool-use fast short-circuit's `sed -n … | head -1` cwd extraction → a `[[ =~ ]]` LAST-match loop, byte-equivalent to the greedy sed on the harness's single-line JSON — pinned by a hazard corpus (Windows `\\`-escaped paths, embedded `"cwd"` decoys inside command strings, spaced forms, cwd-late-position). The rare python-absent fallback keeps its sed (it runs only when python is dead; spawns don't matter there).
- **Dual blind round-1, all folded pre-ship:** static trace (0 Critical, 7 Important — the headline: the turn-gate NEVER skipped in telemetry-active projects because the hook's own auto-analyze tail rewrote four report files after the stamp; pruned + `-mindepth 1`, with a telemetry-fixture proof arm) and execution (1 Critical: the resolver cache was Write-forgeable — the Bash deny held but the `_GUARDED` basename tuple could not express the path, so a forged rc-3 cache flipped a real ui-quality FAIL to SKIP through the gate's own re-derive; closed in the evidence-pattern deny lane, probe-proven DENY). Also folded: stamp now written only when the composed scan actually evaluated (rc 0/1); CRLF-proof cache writes (Windows python); `$0` as a cache input; no `.mega-sdd` minting; measured trade disclosed — the builtin stdin read wins to ~20–30KB and loses above it on macOS (+0.2–0.6s at 200–500KB) while the Windows spawn saving is the tranche's target. Carried open: sub-second mtime races (safe direction), root-child deletion invisibility (over-blocking direction).
- Pinned by `tests/token-efficiency/test-4abc-spawn-tax.sh` (behavioral: zero-python hit + every invalidation class incl. input DELETION, non-trivial multi-vs-singles parity, Stop skip/rescan via state mtimes, the extraction corpus); `tests/hooks/post-tool-use-windows-paths.test.sh`'s exec-count tallies now start cache-cold (the derived cache legitimately broke the proxy's identical-starting-state assumption, not the property it tests). No skill body changed.

## [5.21.0] - 2026-08-01 — tranches 2a/2c/2d: the chain went wave-parallel and the L0 floor became one call

Three levers from [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md) §2a/§2c/§2d, shipped together per the spec's Phase-2 grouping. One blind-dual adversarial round per lane (static-trace on the routing; execution WITH Bash on the wrapper); **2 Criticals killed pre-ship**, both disclosed below.

- **2a — the orchestrated chain now dispatches `execute-bolts --all --parallel`.** The flag, the wave procedure, and the analyze-parallelism auto-run all existed — and no chain path connected them: not one routing row, handoff row, or pipeline example carried `--parallel` (`chain-execution.md`'s "passed to execute-bolts" claim had no channel). Wired now: the three `routing-rules.md` rows, the orchestrate-flow step-6 example, the `handoff-contract.md` generate-units routing row, the `auto-and-memory.md` emission (`["--all", "--parallel", "--auto"]`), and the diagnostics row names the real channel — the in-context `--format=json` `waves` array (the `depends_on` Kahn layering; verified against the script). The **overlap rail stays with the dispatcher** (pairwise-disjoint `target_files` per wave, applied at dispatch — a stale plan cannot bypass it; a plan disagreeing with `units/` is discarded and re-derived). The flag DEFAULT stays off standalone; standalone suggestions deliberately keep plain `--all`. Gain basis: the audit's DAG estimate 2.5–2.7× under the script's own "estimate, not a promise" model — no measured wall-clock is claimed.
  - **Round-1 Critical (static reviewer):** the routing rows had been ported to a SCRIPT in the v5 state-engine work — `routing-rules.md:56` documents `scripts/_lib/state_probes.py`'s output, and the design edited the doc, not the engine: the front door (`/mega-sdd` proposes from `derived.proposed_next`) would have kept dispatching sequential while every prose surface and fixture claimed parallel, with the derive-state fixture pinning the divergence CI-green. Fixed in-round: the engine emits `--all --parallel`, fixture f6 pins it, and the new pin suite pins the ENGINE, not just its docs.
  - **Round-1 rails (same-tree wave concurrency was default-on with unspecified semantics — all specified in `batch-and-fanout.md §--all`):** in-flight cap made concrete (default 5, cap-sized slices; `squad-subagent.md`'s "sensible cap" given the same number); **per-unit gate ranges** under interleaved wave commits = the unit's OWN commit via the atomic-commit identity (`<its-commit>^..<its-commit>`; re-dispatch scans the unit's commit SET so the S7 "never fix-commit-only" purpose survives without attributing sibling commits) — byte-identical to `bolt-base..new-head` under sequential; transient `index.lock` on concurrent commits named CONTENTION in the `bolt-implementer` contract (bounded retry, never `BLOCKED` — a benign race can't manufacture a wave halt); shared-test-state valve disclosed (`--worktree` or drop the flag at Edit; full worktree-coupling REJECTED this round — the flag's merge-back is one line of prose, too thin to carry a default); wave-boundary failure semantics operationalized (in-flight siblings complete their verdict trail — a sibling's fix re-dispatch is remediation within its cap; new units never dispatch past a failure); consumed waves skip completed units (resume-safe); `commands/analyze-parallelism.md` stopped suggesting `--per-squad --parallel` unconditionally (it HALTS on single-squad vaults).
- **2c — the per-bolt L0 fan-out collapsed to ONE call: new `scripts/run-code-gates.sh`.** 9–13 sequential main-thread Bash turns per bolt attempt (resolver block + detect-toolchain + per-tool commands + 4 gate scripts, re-run per panel re-dispatch) → 1. The wrapper composes the five gate scripts **as-is** (every degradation path — gitleaks fallback, semgrep-absent skip, offline-unverified — stays inside them), **short-circuits at the first BLOCKING result** (later gates in `not_run[]`, their subprocesses never spawned — proven by a PATH-shim spy, not JSON trust), and emits ONE merged JSON = the `## Deterministic scan results` payload pasted verbatim per lens. Exit contract 0/1/2 (1 only ever with the `halt` object; crash is 2); `--pack=` honors the pack `## Toolchain` override with visible non-engagement notes; `--no-code-gates`/config skip 1–2/4/6 while **secrets + dep-existence always run**; per-command timeouts kill the whole process group (POSIX); the per-bolt plugin-root resolver block in `code-gates.md` is retired. **Measured:** wrapper floor median **0.637 s/bolt** (tools absent, n = 10); tools-live median 7.06 s/bolt (n = 5), dominated by semgrep's registry pull — a tool cost identical in the flow it replaced.
  - **Round-1 Critical (execution reviewer):** unknown gate exit codes fell through to "pass" — on the documented WindowsApps python-stub environment (the v5.4.0 P0 history) every gate script's bare `python3` exits 49 while the wrapper's own interpreter works, and the reviewer produced a SECRET-bearing diff certified clean at exit 0. Fixed: closed per-gate rc sets (always-run pair → exit 2 on anything undocumented, SAST → visible SKIP), and the test reproduces the stub environment deterministically, asserting exit 2.
  - **Round-1 also folded:** gate-6 vanished from the record on short-circuits without `--unit` → skips prerecorded up front + a tested **gate-accounting invariant** (every gate in exactly one of gates-ran/skips/not_run on every path); nested-`code_gates:` config false-positive → top-level-only, first match wins; failing `fix_cmd` disclosure (`fix_rc`, re-check output), formatter-dirt ownership (controller commits the fix under the unit identity — pre-flight 3 enforces), unresolvable `--unit`/`--pack` visible, `range{}` pins 40-hex SHAs, crash-vs-block exit separation. Windows-only subprocess caveats carried to the Windows lane, named in the spec.
- **2d — `extract-intelligence --max-parallel` default 3 → 5.** Wave batches 6 → 4 at the audit's 18-domain corpus = 1.5× on the agent-batch portion; the superseded "empirical optimum is 3" claim (whose basis — model-assembled per-dispatch overhead — died with 5d's dispatch diet) removed from `predictive-checks.md` along with its garbled fragment; soft-warn >5 and hard cap 8 intact; cap 5 exactly saturates the widest wave (3/4/5/3).
- Pinned by `tests/code-gates/test-run-code-gates.sh` (behavioral: spy-shim short-circuit, stub-interpreter environment, accounting invariant, fix-path disclosure, pack/unit degradation, SHA-pinned range) + `tests/token-efficiency/test-2a2d-chain-parallel.sh` (engine + prose pins, wave rails, 2d supersession). Trigger/e2e fixtures updated to the parallel proposals; scenario-1's pre-existing single-squad `--per-squad` contradiction fixed in passing. Skills: execute-bolts 2.27.0 → 2.28.0, orchestrate-flow 2.17.1 → 2.18.0, extract-intelligence 1.17.0 → 1.18.0, generate-units 2.16.0 → 2.17.0.

## [5.20.0] - 2026-08-01 — tranche 5c: the intent-leg advisor stopped receiving the pasted corpus

`generate-intent` Step 3.7 pasted the drafted 7 vault files + the whole source into the fresh `phase-advisor` subagent — **15–50K tok per dispatch, central ~30K** (research §6.2 row 11's measurement; not re-measured — the pasted-corpus size IS the vault+source size, project-dependent by construction). The P7 slice-first fix had shipped on the **bind leg only** (`.advisor-bundle.md`). Spec: [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md) §5c.

- **The intent leg needs no bundle builder** — after Step 3 every claim, OQ text, and classification bracket the advisor hunts is already ON DISK (the five JSON-only recommend fields ride the Step-3.8 authored patch and are out of this pass's scope). The dispatch carries the vault DIR path + source file PATHS + scope id / phase N-of-total on filtered runs + the OQ roll-up counts, with a **fail-closed pre-dispatch path check** (an unresolvable source → `advisor: unavailable`, never clean) and two carve-outs the adversarial round mandated: on `--scope`/`--phase` runs the on-disk source is UNFILTERED while the vault is deliberately filtered, so sibling-scope/out-of-phase sections are NOT `coverage_gap`s (the old filtered-paste bound, restored as a rule); and MCP-loaded Figma has no on-disk path — it never grounds a `fabrication`, and the dispatcher marks it `unreadable by you` (screenshot FILES stay in scope). A named path that does not open is an `unreadable_source` finding — never an empty findings list the advisor could not earn.
- **The trade, stated:** the removed side is the pasted corpus as main-thread OUTPUT at the 5.0× weight (research's **15–50K/dispatch — GROSS**, project-dependent); the advisor re-reads much of the same corpus as fresh subagent INPUT at 1.0×, so the net is the 5×→1× weight differential plus the selective-read savings — no net constant is quoted. The materialization contract (4 finding types, generate-intent as materializer, the `advisor:` three-state provenance record) is byte-unchanged.
- Pinned by `tests/token-efficiency/test-5c-intent-advisor-seed.sh` — **prose-contract pins** (both legs' expansion contracts, the no-paste dispatch shape, the carve-outs, the fail-closed path check, bind parity, and phase-advisor keeping exactly `Read`/`Grep`/`Glob`); the BEHAVIORAL seed-not-boundary fixture lives on the bind leg (`tests/phase-advisor/test-advisor-bundle.sh`). Skill `generate-intent` 2.13.1 → 2.14.0.

## [5.19.0] - 2026-08-01 — tranche 5b: the codebase map stopped being typed

`codebase-map.md` was model-TYPED in full on every write (measured 37.6K cost-units, ≥188K lifetime) — worst on the `--changed-only` sync lane, whose merge semantics demanded byte-identical carry-forward the model delivered by RETYPING every unchanged row at the 5.0× output weight. New `scripts/derive-codebase-map.sh` assembles the map from a model-written DELTA instead. Spec: [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md) §5b.

- **Division of labour:** the model stays the extraction-output parser and the author of the judgment sections (§5/§6/§7); the script owns the mechanics — §1 tree rendered from Step 4's `files.z` (the model never types the tree), `Last_Scanned_Sha256` joined from Step 5's `hashes.txt` with an in-process hashlib fallback (the model never types 64-hex again), replace-set merge per section, "None detected" schema shape, atomic temp+rename. Parsing tree-sitter capture output in-script is deliberately OUT of scope — no compiled grammars exist on the dev box to verify against (the same reason scan-procedure §Step 5 refuses to change the per-file invocation), and an unverifiable parser would be fabrication-by-code.
- **The 4 prose-trusted anti-hallucination rails are now structural:** (1) carried rows are byte-COPIES with their ORIGINAL sha — pinned by mutating a carried file on disk and asserting the sha does NOT refresh; (2) an absent/corrupt prior in `--mode=merge` is **exit 3 `fallback_full`**, nothing written — the documented full-scan fallback enforced at the exact point the merge happens; (3) prior §2/§4 rows whose `File` vanished are dropped in-process and counted on stdout (§3 deletions ride the model's whole-section replace — `Handler` has no reliable path key); (4) all 7 sections always present, `generated_at`/`generated_by`/`repo_root` script-stamped, and `last_scanned_commit` minted by the script's own `git rev-parse --verify 'HEAD^{commit}'` with the field OMITTED on failure or a literal `HEAD` (the zero-commit poisoning rule enforced where the stamp is minted). Script-owned keys arriving in the delta are ignored WITH a named warning.
- **The write-path gates are chained, so they cannot be skipped:** the deriver runs `secret-scan.sh --redact` on the assembled temp (Step 10a — a planted AWS key is redacted and surfaced in stdout `secret_findings` for the `SECRET-FINDINGS.md` routing; a missing OR malfunctioning redactor is a hard fail-CLOSED exit 2, since `--redact` is documented "exit 0 always" and any other rc means the scanner itself broke — proceeding would ship an unscrubbed credential) and gates the assembly on `validate-codebase-map.sh` **BEFORE the rename** — a rejected map never overwrites a good prior (exit 4 keeps the prior intact, parks the assembly at `<out>.rejected`, and the gate's state scribble is pointed at a system-temp scratch so the canonical state is never poisoned with a verdict about a temp file); validator ERROR (any rc other than 0/1, e.g. its bare python hitting the WindowsApps stub) is a WARNING, not a rejection — a broken validator must never kill a phase-1 chain. Constant spawn count; zero per-item fan-out.
- **Two adversarial rounds folded in (execution reviewer WITH Bash + consumer-drift reviewer; 1 Critical + 1 Critical + 16 Important/Minor):** the silent-§2-wipe class is dead — merge composition is CANONICAL-HEADER-GATED (a variant column order like the in-tree `| Symbol | Signature | File |` fixture is carried verbatim when untouched and exit 3 when touched, never a positional guess); File cells carrying `path.ts:42` citations or backticks are normalized before existence/sha checks; a forgotten `s2.files` derives the replace-set from the delta rows' own File cells instead of failing open into duplicate rows; legacy 4-col priors get an EMPTY-padded sha cell (a minted sha would fake provenance); the two new halt names `codebase_map_derive_failed`/`codebase_map_invalid` enter the catalog, exit 3 is a recorded recovery that takes the sync lane's full-scan-fallback branch, and Step 5's stale "update the sha / drop from §2" instructions were rewritten as delta-producing language. The execution round also caught a regression the rework itself introduced (the prior's `None detected` placeholder leaking above a newly-populated table) — fixed and pinned, along with BOM'd Windows deltas, fence-blind section slicing, and the drop-vs-supersede count order.
- **Measured (synthetic 200-file fixture, method scripted and reproducible; 4 B/tok):** FULL scan — map 37,885 B, model-typed delta 17,365 B ⇒ **54% of the map's bytes no longer typed** (2.18×; ≈5.1K output tok ≈ 25.6K cost-units per full write). MERGE with 5/200 files changed — model types **537 B** against the old full retype of 37,930 B ⇒ **70.6×** on the sync lane (≈9.3K output tok ≈ 46.7K cost-units saved per sync write), which is where the ≥188K lifetime floor lived. The research row's 37.6K/write baseline stands as the pre-change figure.
- **Consumers unchanged:** the schema is byte-compatible (`bind-codebase`, `generate-intent --scan`, the snapshot/freshness machinery untouched); hand-written fixture maps remain valid. `scan-procedure.md` §Incremental + Step 10/10a rewritten to the delta contract; SKILL.md carries the runnable form. Pinned by `tests/token-efficiency/test-derive-codebase-map.sh` (rails 1–4 each proven behaviorally, secret-scan chain, prose pins). Skill `scan-codebase` 2.21.0 → 2.22.0.

## [5.18.0] - 2026-08-01 — tranche 5d: the extract-dispatch invariant moved to where the controller can't drop it

`extract-intelligence`'s controller used to TYPE, into every one of ~15 wave-subagent dispatches, the full invariant extraction contract (DISCIPLINE deltas + EXTRACTION DEPTH + DEEP DISCIPLINES P1–P4/P6 + REPORT BACK + both self-check rails + the glossary-usage block) plus two computed injections (`<STACK_IDIOM_ROWS>`, `<GLOSSARY_INDEX>`). Spec: [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md) §5d.

- **The invariant now rides `agents/domain-extractor.md`** — the subagent's system prompt: loads on every `domain-extractor` dispatch by construction (extract-intelligence is its sole dispatcher; a hurried controller can no longer truncate the contract — delivery moved UP the gates>rules ladder), byte-identical across all dispatches (the strongest cache position), typed by no one. Relocation is 1:1 with only positional references reworded; pinned block-by-block by the rewritten `tests/token-efficiency/test-b1-wave-dispatch-diet.sh` and `tests/fixtures/iter80-extract-deepening/verify.sh`.
- **The injections are now script-derived**: new `scripts/build-extract-static.sh` writes `<kb>/.dispatch-static.md` (Wave 0, re-run after the Wave 1 gate; atomic temp+rename; fail-closed exit 2; constant spawn count). It PARSES the MASTER STACK IDIOM TABLE out of `wave-dispatch-templates.md` at run time — the single-copy rule is now structural, not a convention — and every B1 slicing rule (UNION, full-table fallback, mapped-columns-only mixes, never-empty, kb-leak-scan LANG_MAP alias parity, markup-ignored) is preserved and script-enforced. The glossary `short_def` becomes a verbatim word-boundary prefix — the model-paraphrase surface of the old model-built index is gone. The file sits at the KB root, deliberately outside `kb-leak-scan.sh`'s `SCAN_DIRS` (raw idiom tokens must never count as tech-leaks). New `tests/token-efficiency/test-extract-dispatch-static.sh` pins parse/slice/gloss/atomicity/exclusion.
- **Measured (against the shipped tree, 4 B/tok both sides):** skeleton fence 9,836 B → 772 B = **9,064 B/dispatch** the controller no longer types — a FLOOR, since the old flow additionally typed the idiom slice (820–920 B measured) and the glossary index (project-dependent, ×12) on top. Fence term alone per 15-dispatch run: ≈ **34.0K output tok ≈ 170K cost-units at the 5.0× output weight**, plus ~3.1–3.4K tok of slice; the index term is published as a formula, not an invented constant. The 80–120 KB glossary read leaves the MAIN thread entirely (the script reads it in a subprocess). **The research row's ~150K/run (~129K output) estimate did NOT reproduce and is superseded by these measurements** — same rule as Phase 0: the measured number replaces the estimate, whichever way it moves.
- **The trade, stated:** the agent body grew +10,331 B (≈2.6K tok) of subagent INPUT — worst corner (15× cache-miss at 1.25×) ~48K cost-units vs the 170K output-side floor it replaces (~3.5×); realistic corner (1 creation + 14 cache-reads) ~6.8K (~25×). The per-subagent `.dispatch-static.md` Read is a near-wash (same bytes previously arrived as prompt input). Wall-clock: ~34K tok of serial main-thread generation removed ≈ 3.8–11.3 min per extract run at 50–150 tok/s.
- **Adversarial round folded in (2 blind reviewers, 24 findings — all triaged, the load-bearing ones fixed):** the Wave-1 gate now HARD-fails on a glossary with zero `## <term>` headings (the post-Wave-1 index gate would otherwise dead-end the whole run with no remediable cause — the fix lands where the re-dispatch remedy still applies, and Agent 1.A's SCOPE now mandates the heading format); Wave 0 builds with `--no-glossary` so a re-run into an existing KB can never hand Wave-1 subagents a stale prior-run index; the master-table parse is anchored on the `MASTER STACK IDIOM TABLE` marker (an illustrative slice example elsewhere in the doc cannot hijack it) and DIES on column-name drift instead of silently reverting to the full table while claiming a scan-meta detection; UTF-8 is explicit on every read and the write (a curly quote under a Windows ANSI-locale python crashed the write as an uncaught `UnicodeEncodeError` and orphaned the temp file); fenced-code `## ` lines no longer truncate the previous term's spot-read range; the hard-coded 9-row expectation became ≥1-uniform-rows (a legitimate tenth idiom row would have bricked every extraction at Wave 0 — the shipped 9-row shape stays pinned by the b1 test, where a change fails visibly in CI instead); the "loads by construction" claim is qualified (conditional on dispatching `domain-extractor`, its sole dispatcher); and the new test pins every real parse branch with doctored master tables plus full alias-set parity against `kb-leak-scan.sh`, not two sampled aliases.
- **Nothing else moved:** every between-wave gate, wave structure, Wave-5 main-thread rule, model tiers, secret-scan, scorecard, handoff. Amended into the tech-agnostic spec (2026-08-01, under the B1 amendment). Skill `extract-intelligence` 1.16.1 → 1.17.0.

## [5.17.0] - 2026-08-01 — tranche 3a: the per-OQ walk collapsed to ONE prompt

`resolve-oq`'s standard walk cost three human round trips per answered OQ (action menu → answer → destination confirm). It now costs one: **the choice IS the answer and IS the destination confirmation.** Spec: [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md) §3a. Canonical shape: `skills/resolve-oq/references/interactive-walk.md` Step 2b — `recommendation-context.md` now points at it instead of restating it, so the prompt has exactly one normative home.

- **The shipped prompt:** `[1]` recommended answer — its description carries the keterangan AND discloses where the answer lands (target doc, inline vs promoted, cross-cutting cross-refs), so choosing it also confirms the destination · `[2]` Skip (this OQ only; the walk continues) · `[3]` Defer · `[4]` Out of scope · "Other" = the free-text answer, composable with a trailing `→ <file>.md` destination override — a BARE override accepts the recommendation and re-lands it, and must never parse as Skip (that silently discarded an accepted answer before this round) · **Esc = end the walk**, the plugin-wide reading of Esc (`halt-recovery.md`, `propose-and-confirm-prompt.md`) — Skip got a real slot precisely so Esc could keep that meaning. **No typed `STOP` sentinel** — an OQ like "lanjut atau berhenti?" is answerable with the word "stop", which a sentinel would swallow.
- **The sibling spec was reconciled, not copied.** `recommendation-context.md` already specified one prompt — at FIVE options, over the platform's 4-option cap, with Skip dropped. The considered alternatives lost their slot and ride the question text as sourced prose (`Alternatif: … — kalau …`, each with its citation or an explicit `tanpa sumber` marker; no grounded alternative → the line is omitted, never padded — the cap is a ceiling, not a quota). The `--binding` propagated walk uses the same collapsed prompt minus Defer (nested deferral unsupported) — three contiguous options (`[1]` recommended answer / `[2]` Skip / `[3]` Out of scope), never a fourth invented to fill the freed capacity.
- **Defer stays 2 stops by design, not 3:** the follow-up is ONE `AskUserQuestion` CALL carrying TWO questions (sub-target + reason) — the 4-option cap is per QUESTION, and a call takes 1–4 of them. Recorded state (`defer_to`, `deferred_reason`, the OOS rationale) is still ASKED, never defaulted or derived (invariant #5); greenfield drops only the sub-target question and `defer_to` takes its schema default `stakeholder`. Esc on a follow-up abandons that outcome — never a canned reason.
- **Gain, re-derived from the shipped procedure** (closed form under `--auto`: `stops = N + #Defer + #OOS` — Answer and Skip cost 1, Defer and OOS cost 2 because their second value is recorded state): at the stated mix (N=8: 5 Answer / 1 Defer / 1 OOS / 1 Skip) **21 → 10 stops (2.1×)**; interactive **23 → 12** first pass / **24 → 13** on resume (**1.85–1.92×**). One correction to the published band: **7 is unreachable at N=8** — the human must see the prompt even to decline it, so the collapsed floor is exactly N and the band is **8–13**, not 7–13. The delivered work meets the published claim at the stated mix; the ceiling was NOT widened (an earlier 13→16 draft is retracted as a target adjusted to the implementation).
- **What the collapse did NOT move:** the mandated keterangan contract on every prompt (question text + source + per-option explanation, Indonesian default with standing language precedence); the high-stakes `⚠️` marker in BOTH positions (panel banner + recommended option's description prefix); the derive contract — recorded `action` letters `A`/`B`/`C` are unchanged, slot numbers are display only, Skip emits no event at all; `--auto` still never auto-decides a substance prompt (the skips remain logistical only). Esc-terminated rounds are now first-class: Step 4's no-drop check is scoped to OQs that were PRESENTED, and Step 5 reports `{N} unreached` distinct from `{S}` skipped and `{U}` scope-filtered.
- **Tests:** `tests/interaction-keterangan/test-oq-single-prompt.sh` (new — pins the verbatim template, the no-recommendation shape, the "Other" parse order, the Esc/Skip split, the one-normative-home rule, the exact derive strings, cross-surface consistency across SKILL.md / binding-mode / `--auto`, the prompt budget, and the double high-stakes marker); behavior fixture `tests/skill-triggering/resolve-oq.test.md` rewritten off the letter menu (B1–B15); `test-oq-prompt-keterangan.sh` updated. Skill `resolve-oq` 2.7.1 → 2.9.0.

## [5.16.0] - 2026-07-31 — tranche 2b: deterministic bolt-dispatch-prompt builder

`scripts/build-dispatch-prompt.sh` replaces model-assembled dispatch-prompt prose. Spec: [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md) §2b. Contract: [`plugins/mega-sdd/skills/execute-bolts/references/context-enrichment.md`](plugins/mega-sdd/skills/execute-bolts/references/context-enrichment.md).

**The token figures are MEASURED, against shipped code.** Three different headline numbers were published across three review rounds because each round re-derived from the previous round's measurement instead of from the code, and the code moved under all of them (stdout slim, `--plugin-root` made mandatory, the design slice moved off stdout). All three are struck. A dedicated measurement pass then ran LAST over the working tree — 105 runs, 35 real units on three fixture projects, three arms discriminated deterministically rather than assumed, every byte figure stated at a named absolute-path length because path length is what made the earlier figures non-reproducible. Method and full tables: spec §2b.

- **Output, OLD side: ~179K tok per 40-unit run — confirmed, and unaffected by any of this.** It describes the flow being replaced (the ~9KB prompt materialized TWICE as model output).
- **Output, NEW side: 578–1,251 B/bolt ⇒ `14.3×–31.0×`,** centre of mass `17.4×–24.2×`. The residual is an enumerated set (invocation string / `inline_core` / `subagent_type` / the two tool `description` params), so it can be audited term by term rather than as a sum. **It is a deliberate LOWER bound** — the new side counts the `description` params, the 179K old side counted prompt bytes only. Two of the six terms are labelled ESTIMATED because no shipped literal exists to measure.
- **The design-lens hand-off is its own line item, never folded into that ratio:** greenfield **9,635 B → 129 B ≈ 2,377 output tok saved per UI bolt** (≈823 tok when the truncation rung has fired; only ≈92 tok on a starterkit repo, which must not be quoted as the headline). ≈**8,200–23,800 output tok** per 40-unit run.
- **Input is a separate line item and is NOT netted against output.** Builder stdout, min/med/max at this repo's root: non-UI **731 / 815 / 922 B**; UI-starterkit **985 / 1,043 / 1,343 B**; UI-greenfield **1,199 / 1,256 / 1,343 B** ⇒ ~8,150–9,252 input tok/run. **No cost-weighted ratio is claimed in either direction** — the OLD flow's input debit was never quantified, and a one-sided weighting would be a fabricated comparison.

**Round 4 does not move those figures** (it changed no term in the residual and no `inline_core` byte), with one disclosed exception in the good direction: gating the lens-input write on `ui_bearing` removes ~103–157 B of stdout from every non-UI bolt **on a starterkit repo** — a case the non-UI arm could not measure, because its fixture carries no `starterkit-context.yaml`. Separately, round 4 grew the emitted FILE by **two** constants — neither is a billed channel, and §Size check carries both: **+235 B on every unit** (the tracker's corrected enumeration) and a further **+216 B on non-`ui_bearing` units only** (the new `design_slice_path` omission line in the PROVENANCE appendix — invariant #5 requires the absence to carry a recorded reason, and a reason has bytes). The non-UI arm is the larger corpus (25 of 35 measured units), so its gap range shifts by **+451 B**, not +235.

**A published derivation was WITHDRAWN, not quietly deleted.** The cap amendment argued the `dispatch_prompt_too_large` halt's reachability algebraically, via "all priorities 1–8 at floor ⟹ `t2 ≥ cap_t2 + 1`". That implication is false: `all_1_to_8_at_floor` is evaluated over the sections actually added, and a unit with no priority-1..8 section satisfies it **vacuously**, at any size. Reachability itself was never in doubt — it is established empirically, on four units plus a lean probe with a working non-vacuity control — so the algebra is struck and the empirical table carries the claim. Struck with it: the `cap_hard ≤ 12906` ceiling (built on a T2 floor that does not exist) and a backlog item proposing to drop the halt's `total > cap_hard` term, which would have turned the halt into a chain-killer on any unit citing a single constitution clause.

**The blind-lens rail is now scoped to its purpose: *no lens may receive a path that reaches another lens's verdict or the implementer's self-report.*** The previous absolute form ("no path into `<vault>/bolts/U-XXX/` in any form") was falsified by the shipping procedure two paragraphs below it, which routes design-lens screenshots to `<bolt-dir>/views`. `<vault>/bolts/U-XXX/` stays forbidden — it holds `bolt-report.md`. A new sibling, **`<vault>/lens-inputs/U-XXX/`, holds controller-written lens inputs and NOTHING else, ever**; that invariant is what makes it safe, not the path string.

**The design slice moved off stdout** to `<vault>/lens-inputs/U-XXX/design-slice.md`, returned as `design_slice_path` (formerly `design_slice_text`). It was being billed twice per greenfield UI bolt — once inbound as builder stdout, once outbound as the controller's verbatim paste into the lens prompt — and the outbound leg was the larger term and appeared in no published line item.

**Round 4 — the reconciliation pass.** Rounds 1–3 ran the script lane, the markdown lane and the measurement lane CONCURRENTLY and never reconciled their handoffs, which produced the same defect three times: prose asserting a breach the code had closed, or a guarantee the code did not give. Round 4 was authored sequentially over every surface — code, then every document against that code, then this entry.

- **CRITICAL — the spec mandated a catastrophe the code had deleted.** §Builder contract requirement 2 was a normative MUST that the target *"must be **unlinked before assembly begins**"*, and four more shipped sites asserted its consequence ("no — target unlinked"). Round 3 had deleted `_unlink_target()` precisely because that pre-assembly unlink turned a stdout encoding error on the Windows target into *the builder deleting the correct dispatch prompt it had just written*. A maintainer following `plugins/mega-sdd/CLAUDE.md` ("every behavior change should trace to a spec section") would have restored it as a compliance fix. All six sites now state the shipped guarantee — publication is temp-file + atomic rename, on exit 2 and exit 4 an existing correct prompt is left INTACT and unchanged, and **the exit code is the discriminator, never the file's existence**. Requirement 2 now FORBIDS a pre-assembly unlink and says why.
- **`design_slice_path` and the lens-input FILE were written for units that are not UI-bearing.** The write was gated on the slice TEXT, and on the starterkit branch that text exists for any unit whose frontmatter carries `starterkit_relevance: [ui_ux]` — reproduced live on a unit whose only target was `app/Services/Settlement.php`. It is now gated on `ui_bearing`, the same gate the `design_slice` section already used; the two disagreed and the looser one decided what hit disk. `review-panel.md`'s "pure-backend units never pay for it" is true as a result. The unit's own starterkit slice still ships into the prompt — the fix withholds a lens input, it does not delete content.
- **A `[LOCKED]` entry above a filler row was SILENTLY dropped.** The round-3 structural header drop treated ANY dash-only row as a `|---|` delimiter and discarded the row before it as a header, so a real locked path sitting above `| - | - | - | - |` vanished from `DO NOT MODIFY:` — and unlike the no-delimiter case, nothing was recorded. The delimiter test is now positional (a markdown alignment row is the table's SECOND row, never a later one; the KB schema puts exactly one table per section), and every row the parser discards is recorded with a reason. **Dropping a LOCKED entry silently is the same invariant-#5 class as inventing one.**
- **`compose()` warned that a value was a placeholder token and then rendered it anyway** — producing exactly what the comment eight lines above calls "worse than an omitted line", and satisfying `design_system_not_injected` vacuously (reproduced: `vault.json` `design_system.palette: "None"` → the prompt read `palette=None`). **Decided as OMIT**: invariant #5 says absent input is omitted with a recorded reason, and a source that says "None" supplied no palette. The pair is dropped, the warning survives (the source is what to fix), the omission is recorded, and the gate now fires honestly. The detector remains a smoke alarm — a warning, never an exit code — and still cannot fire on prose containing the word.
- **The in-prompt budget tracker's enumeration did not sum.** It attributed "the tier banners" to the `file_total` − `total` gap, but the title and `TIER 1` banners are appended into `t1` and are already inside `consumed_t1`. The gap is **exactly four blocks plus the blank lines joining them: the `TIER 2` banner, the tracker block itself, the `TIER 3` pointer list, and the `PROVENANCE — omissions` appendix** — of the tier banners, only `TIER 2` is outside, which is not the same claim. Corrected in all four places (builder tracker, builder stdout comment, `context-enrichment.md` template, `bolt-dispatch-prompt.md` template). The identity was re-verified live and sums to the byte.
- **Every document was reconciled to the shipped code, in both directions** — prose publishing a fixed defect as open, and prose promising a guarantee the code does not give. Five §Known open rows named defects this same tranche had already fixed and are gone, with what each asserted recorded under the table.

**Round 5 — the closing lens ran the repo's OWN CI command, and it was red.** Every earlier round ran the two new suites and called it green. The closing pass instead ran `.github/workflows/tests.yml`'s find-and-loop over **both** test trees and got `OVERALL_RC=1` — the exact lesson this repo already recorded once (v4.61.0: run BOTH trees, or `gh run list`, before claiming green). Two tracked, unmodified suites passed on `HEAD` and failed on the tranche:

- **`tests/platform/test-platform-pins.sh` P6 — and it was a real defect, not a stale pin.** The new §Invocation block put the plugin-root environment variable into a `references/*.md` file, where this repo's own rule says it is **not substituted** (`generate-intent/references/vault-contract.md`). A controller that loaded the contract file and copied the canonical invocation would have emitted `bash "/scripts/build-dispatch-prompt.sh" … --plugin-root=""` and written no prompt for that bolt. The reference now carries a `<plugin-root>` placeholder plus the reason, and points at `SKILL.md` §Step 4.5 for the runnable form — which is also the string the measurement quotes.
- **`tests/boilerplate-diet/test-p2d-agent-contracts.sh` — two pins the tranche deliberately invalidated.** Both were **inverted, not deleted**: the retired order-3 legacy-dispatch element is now pinned as *closed*, asserted in the template **and** the bridge, so a later pass cannot quietly restore a fallback that would tell a generic executor its system prompt carries contracts it does not hold; and `cap_t1` is pinned to its amended `12288` **alongside the amendment itself**, so the number cannot drift without its justification. `cap_hard` and `cap_t2` stay pinned unchanged — they are the load-bearing ones (halt term (b), and the cascade trigger).

**Round 4's own fix had opened the opposite direction, and round 5 closed both.** Making the table-header drop structural stopped a real `[LOCKED]` entry being eaten by a filler row — but a `data-mutation-policy.md` section carrying **two** tables then shipped the second table's header cell as a `DO NOT MODIFY:` path with a genuine citation. The real bug was upstream of both: the parser flattened the section into one row list, destroying the only structure that answers the question. Rows are now grouped into **contiguous pipe blocks** — in markdown a table ends at its first blank line, so one block is one table and each gets its own header/alignment pair skipped. **Every** valid block is read, not just the first: dropping a second table's genuine `[LOCKED]` path would silently under-lock, which is the same invariant-#5 class as inventing one, pointed the other way. A block with no `|---|` second row is still skipped whole and recorded — fail closed. Pinned by `W10`/`W11`, which assert both directions on one fixture.

**Known open — every row re-verified against shipped code in round 4** (details and rationale in `context-enrichment.md §Known open`):

| # | what | why not here |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The pack-resolver call sites outside the builder still swallow non-zero exits** — re-counted in round 4 as **11 call sites across 7 validators** (`validate-flow-coverage` ×4, `validate-sibling-consistency` ×2, and one each in `validate-fanout-parity`, `validate-unit-spec`, `validate-ui-quality`, `validate-cross-cutting-registration`, `validate-ui-deferral`), every one of the form `X=$(…) \|\| X=""`. Round 3 recorded "6"; that figure was never right. On the Windows target the App-Execution-Alias `python3` stub makes "resolver failed" indistinguishable from "packless project", so those gates go dark at exit 0. The discrimination contract is honoured by the builder and `validate-dispatch-prompt.sh` only. | Pre-existing; spans seven scripts including PreToolUse-blocking gates. Deserves its own change with its own portability tests. |
| 2 | **`hooks/post-tool-use` decodes `BASH_CMD_B64` three times per Bash event** (one Python decode + two `base64 -d` pipes). One decode into a shell variable would serve all three consumers; at ~220 ms/spawn on the CrowdStrike laptops that is a real per-Bash-call cost. | Consolidating them touches a hot path that fires on every Bash call in every session; regression risk there outweighs the saving in this tranche. |
| 3 | A moat test still pins the struck `12906` ceiling as a regression rail (cascade suite, assertion I4). | Deleting an assertion with no code change to justify it is not something a review round should do. Re-ground it by measurement or drop it in a change that says so. |
| 4 | The reported `total` covers T1+T2 only — the `PROVENANCE` appendix, the `TIER 2` banner, the tracker block and the `TIER 3` banner + pointer list sit outside it and outside the truncation cascade. Round 4 fixed what the enumeration SAYS and added `file_total`; neither changes what the cascade COUNTS. | Closing it is a builder change (budget the appendix into the cascade, or make on-disk size the budgeted figure). |
| 5 | Design-lens screenshots still land in `<bolt-dir>/views`; they belong under `lens-inputs/U-XXX/views/` by the same reasoning that moved the design slice. | Touches `capture-views.sh --out` and the procedure that calls it. |
| 6 | **NEW, found in round 4:** a failed run can now leave a STALE prompt the advisory validator passes. `hooks/post-tool-use` fires `validate-dispatch-prompt.sh` on any Bash call matching `build-dispatch-prompt.sh` **regardless of exit code**, and the validator globs `bolts/*/dispatch-prompt.md` with **no freshness check** — so after exit 2/4 it can read the previous attempt and write a passing state where the unlink used to make it go dark. This is the second-order cost of removing the unlink, and removing it was still correct. | The fix is a freshness signal (the hook already knows `BASH_EXIT_CODE`), **not** a return of the unlink — that would trade a stale advisory state for a destroyed artifact. Advisory surface only: no dispatch decision reads this state. |
| 7 | `## Prior failure context` (Reflexion into the dispatch) is specified and not implemented — the spec obligation was restored and the gap recorded rather than the spec loosened. **This one is carried in `context-enrichment.md §Named backlog`, not §Known open** (it is an unbuilt obligation, not a defect in shipped behavior). | |

## [5.15.0] - 2026-07-31

feat(fork-readiness): `scan-codebase` and `bind-codebase` are now non-interactive on every path — and the spawn-cost gate stopped being a chain-killer on the way.

Groups B and C of the fork-safety audit's edit list ([`research/2026-07-30-fork-safety-audit-scan-bind.md`](research/2026-07-30-fork-safety-audit-scan-bind.md)). **No `context: fork` frontmatter was flipped** — that step stays gated on the two interactive runs the spec specifies. Everything that makes the flip *safe* ships here. Skill versions: `scan-codebase` 2.21.0, `bind-codebase` 2.13.0.

**The spawn-cost gate needed a third lane, and finding that out took two tries.** Converting the `>60s` `AskUserQuestion` into a hard blocker made the skill fork-safe and simultaneously introduced a live regression: the old `--auto` *proceeded*, the new behaviour was a hard STOP at phase 1 of nearly every brownfield chain, with **zero** orchestrate-flow routing rows able to pre-resolve it. On Windows (~0.22 s/spawn) that fires at ~272 files. Restoring "proceed" was not the answer either — that re-opens the unattended multi-hour stall the gate exists to close (100k files ≈ 6.1 h). The gate now resolves by lane, first match wins:

| lane | condition | outcome |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | explicit `--engine=` / `--include=` | proceed, log the estimate — the caller already decided |
| 2 | undecided **standalone** | `scan_spawn_budget_exceeded`, STOP, carrying the exact re-run commands |
| 3 | **unattended** (orchestrator-dispatched or forked) | downgrade to regex, **recorded in three places** |

Lane 3 stamps `precision_tier: regex` plus a new `precision_downgrade_reason` in the map frontmatter, emits one chat line, and carries the AST-recovery command in the handoff `next_action.rationale`. This follows the house rule that `--auto` takes the SAFEST option (precedent: `generate-units/references/pagerank-targeting.md` §`--auto` policy) — unattended, "safest" is neither a six-hour stall nor a phase-1 halt. It does **not** breach the no-silent-downgrade rail: as that same precedent puts it, *"'silently' is about the record, not the action"* — and the record here is stronger than the prompt it replaces.

**A flag-literal lane 3 would have re-created the regression inside its own fix.** `--auto` looks like the obvious condition, but **no routing row renders it on the scan hop** — scan is phase 1, so nothing hands it a handoff. Keying lane 3 on the flag would have dropped every chain-dispatched scan straight back into lane 2. The condition is unattended-ness, with an explicit fail-safe tie-break toward lane 3: a stamped regex map is recoverable, a halted chain produced nothing.

**scan-codebase (Group B).** Every human-stop path became a named blocker carrying its re-run command — `scan_spawn_budget_exceeded`, `scan_repo_too_large`, `scan_primary_app_ambiguous` — with YAML shapes reusing the file's shipped form. The monorepo prompt ("ask ONCE which app is the PRIMARY scan target") became deterministic precedence: explicit `--include` > owning root manifest > single app-root manifest, blocker only on residual ambiguity. `0 public interfaces` is explicitly **not** a halt — a repo may legitimately expose nothing — and is recorded as a suggested re-run instead. **Handoff emission is now unconditional**: it was gated on `--auto`, which a direct `/mega-sdd:scan-codebase` invocation never injects, so a forked run would have emitted no `next_action`, no `artifacts[]`, no `blockers[]` at all. And the secret-scan finding now lands on disk (`.mega-sdd/codebase/SECRET-FINDINGS.md`, `file:line` + pattern class, never the matched value) — the map only ever stored `[REDACTED-SECRET]`, so the live credential's location existed **only in chat** and a fork would have swallowed the one thing needed to rotate it.

**bind-codebase (Group C).** A deterministic Step 0 resolves the vault from `--vault=`/positional → `derive-state.sh`'s `derived.vault_path` → a CWD probe, and **ambiguity emits `bind_inputs_missing` with the candidate list — never `vaults[0]`, never a guess, never a prompt.** The resolved path is constrained to a **direct child** of `.mega-sdd/vaults/`, matching the four non-recursive globs the gate actually scans; "under" would have let a nested path resolve somewhere the gate never looks. `conflict-resolution.md`'s *"bind-codebase prompts user to confirm; vault is patched in place"* was stale attribution — resolve-oq's walker owns both — and under fork it had no compliant reading left, since the only way to follow it was to patch without the confirm, which the same file forbids. Reattributed. Bind's handoff is unconditional too, for the same reason as scan's.

**The moat was not touched, by design and by verification.** The audit established that bind's CONFLICT/OQ decision path is already fork-clean and needs zero edits; an adversarial verifier confirmed nothing in this change reaches it. All the work is at the input and output boundaries.

**Tests:** `plugins/mega-sdd/tests/scan/test-scan-codebase-fork.sh` and `plugins/mega-sdd/tests/moat/test-bind-codebase-fork.sh` — both structured to pass today and to *fire* once the frontmatter flips, so the flip cannot land without its rails. The ask-class sweep matches paraphrases, not just the literal `AskUserQuestion` token, with legitimate mentions allow-listed by exact context so it cannot become a rubber stamp. Suite: 179 pass / 0 fail.

## [5.14.0] - 2026-07-31

fix(sync-lane): the Mode-D fallback handed a non-interactive `bind-codebase` no vault at all — found while auditing whether scan/bind can be forked.

Phase 5a of [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md) asked a narrow question: can `scan-codebase` and `bind-codebase` take `context: fork`? A 13-agent audit (6 dimensions, each adversarially refuted) answered it — and found a live defect on the way. Full report with `file:line` evidence: [`research/2026-07-30-fork-safety-audit-scan-bind.md`](research/2026-07-30-fork-safety-audit-scan-bind.md).

**The live defect — five surfaces, one missing word.** On the Mode-D sync lane, `scan-codebase --changed-only`'s full-scan fallback writes **no** `.sync-changed-paths.txt` and hands straight to a FULL re-bind. On that branch the vault path is the **only** signal the downstream bind receives — and bind is non-interactive there, so it cannot ask for it. The authoritative handoff schema always rendered it as `bind-codebase <vault> --auto`; the design spec's shorthand rendered it bare, and three operative surfaces had copied the shorthand. Fixed in all five, including the spec that was the drift source. Pinned by the new `tests/scan/test-sync-lane-vault-signal.sh`, which caught two of the five sites the first time it ran — one of them a site this change had already missed.

**Fork attribution corrected.** Three surfaces described `bind-codebase` as already `context: fork` while its frontmatter carries no `context:` key. A doc that claims a skill is forked when it is not is how the real flip gets skipped; the test now fails if that reappears, and flips its assertions to demand the Group-C rails the moment `context: fork` does land.

**The audit's answer, recorded in the spec so it is not re-litigated:**

- **`scan-codebase` — GO** after a bounded edit set. **`bind-codebase` — NO-GO** until one probe returns. Both additionally gated by the project's own precondition (`plugins/mega-sdd/CLAUDE.md:69`): fork may extend to scan/bind only after the `detect-drift` token A/B confirms the win, which has **never produced a verdict** — the only attempt failed because `context: fork` silently no-ops under `claude -p`.
- **The risk everyone assumed is not the risk.** "A fork could move where `.validation-blockers.json` lands and break the CONFLICT gate" is **refuted** on three independent mechanisms: bind never writes that file (sole writer `scripts/validate-handoff-binding-units.sh:85`, invoked by hooks with a hook-computed `--cwd`); the state is recomputed at the gate before it is read (`hooks/pre-tool-use:421-422` → `:474-494`, fail-closed); direct writes are hard-denied. **The moat is fork-immune here — by recompute-at-gate, not by anything a skill body says.** bind's CONFLICT verdict path needs zero edits, and any patch that touches it while "making it fork-safe" should be rejected on sight.
- **The real blocker cannot be fixed by editing prose.** `bind-codebase` dispatches `phase-advisor` by default. Whether a `context: fork` body can dispatch an `Agent` at depth 2 is doc-cited but never exercised, and the `detect-drift` pilot cannot settle it — detect-drift dispatches no subagent at all. A permanent `--no-advisor` fallback is not the answer: that is a silent moat-recall downgrade.
- **Two design decisions recorded** (neither is inferable from any existing rail): the spawn-cost gate becomes a named blocker carrying its re-run command rather than an automatic downgrade to regex — `scan-procedure.md:240` puts the precision choice with the user, and a blocker keeps it there; and the monorepo rail becomes deterministic precedence with a blocker only on residual ambiguity, because **zero** orchestrate-flow routing rows carry `--include`/`--engine`, so a bare blocker would convert a one-time question into a phase-1 chain halt for every monorepo user.
- Also corrected: "one missing `--auto-policy` paragraph another skill already ships verbatim" was wrong twice over — verbatim reuse is *incorrect* here, since `generate-units` can default to `--skip-pagerank` only because PageRank is advisory, while scan's extraction **is** the deliverable.

No frontmatter was flipped. That step is gated on two interactive runs (a token/handoff/CWD pilot, and a depth-2 `Agent` probe) which are specified in the spec.

## [5.13.0] - 2026-07-30

fix(telemetry): the token instrument was wrong by ~11.6x in one direction and ~2.5x in the other — every prior optimization round was aimed with it.

This ships **Phase 0** of [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-token-and-latency-optimization.md) (research: [`research/2026-07-30-token-audit-end-to-end.md`](research/2026-07-30-token-audit-end-to-end.md)). It saves **zero tokens by itself** — it is the ruler the rest of the work is measured with, and it was mis-calibrated three separate ways.

**⚠️ Expect the numbers to MOVE. That is the correction landing, not a regression.** Main-thread cost jumps ~11x, subagent cost drops ~2.5x, and cache-creation cost rises ~60% on the main lane. Nothing got more expensive; the instrument stopped lying.

**1. `hooks/stop` recorded one message per turn, not the turn.** It kept only `last_usage` — but a turn is many assistant messages (one per tool round trip), and the last carries only the final message's usage. `hooks/subagent-stop` already carried this correction for the subagent lane, its own comment noting last-message-only ran "~7x under"; the main lane never got it. Measured over **10 real session transcripts / 547 turns**: last-message-only under-reports by **11.65x aggregate** (median 9.53x, range 2.8–18.3x).

**The obvious fix would have been worse than the bug.** "Sum the transcript" is right for `subagent-stop` — a subagent transcript is read *once*, at the subagent boundary. It is wrong for `stop`: the main transcript is append-only for the whole session and the hook fires *every turn*, so summing the file each time re-emits the cumulative total every turn — O(N²). `stop` now windows by a **per-session byte cursor** and sums only what was appended since its last firing. Per-session matters: one shared cursor would see a path mismatch under two concurrent sessions, reset, and re-sum each whole transcript. Truncation skips to the new EOF rather than re-counting from zero — for an instrument, one under-reported turn beats one fabricated spike. A double `Stop` firing now correctly emits `{}` instead of re-billing the turn.

**2. Both lanes over-counted by ~2.5x — the harness writes one JSONL record per content block.** All records for one message share a `message.id` and repeat the *same* usage object (verified: 0 of 56 duplicated ids carried divergent usage in a 548-record transcript). Blind summing therefore inflates by the block multiplicity — **2.468x aggregate**, median 2.404x. Both hooks now dedup by `message.id`. Dedup keys on a **non-empty** id only: a message with no id has no identity to dedup on, and collapsing those silently drops distinct turns (caught by the existing subagent fixture, whose messages carry no ids). `stop` additionally skips `isSidechain` records — those are billed by `SubagentStop` against their own nested transcript, so counting them in the main lane would double-count the entire bolt and review-panel lane.

**3. `report-token-cost.sh` priced ALL cache creation at the 5-minute rate.** It hard-coded x1.25; the 1-hour TTL costs x2.00. Cache creation is the single largest line item in a real pipeline run, so the report understated it by 60% wherever the 1h TTL applied.

The planned fix was a per-lane constant. Measurement made that unnecessary: **the transcript states the TTL per message** in `usage.cache_creation.ephemeral_{5m,1h}_input_tokens` (measured this session: 666,802 tokens @1h, 0 @5m on the main lane — confirming the 1h claim as a *measurement*, not an assumption). Both hooks now carry that split into telemetry and the report prices cache creation **exactly**. The lane constants survive only as the fallback for telemetry written before this release, a partial split charges its residual rather than dropping it, and **every report states what share was measured vs assumed** — `cache_creation_ttl.pct_measured`. The existing telemetry in a repo is 0% measured, so the first genuinely measured baseline is the first run after this release, not a re-report of old data.

**Free rider:** the same read captures `message.model`, so the report now carries a **By model** table. That answers "what tier did the bolt lane actually run at" from ordinary telemetry — the spec's Phase 1b no longer needs a special instrumented run.

**Tests:** new `plugins/mega-sdd/tests/token-cost/test-stop-turn-usage.sh` (14 assertions: window, dedup, id-less messages, sidechain exclusion, per-session cursor isolation, truncation-resets-to-EOF, malformed-transcript-still-exits-0) plus TTL-exact / lane-fallback / residual cases in `test-token-cost-report.sh`.

## [5.12.0] - 2026-07-30

fix(windows): five findings from a full-plugin Windows portability audit — including a missing `.gitattributes` that makes a default Git-for-Windows clone unable to execute a single script.

A six-finder sweep over the whole transitive closure (`SKILL.md` → `references/` → `scripts/` → `_lib/` → `hooks/`, ~300 files) produced **27 findings that survived adversarial refutation** — 4 `hang`, 7 `silent-wrong`, 13 `degraded`, 3 `cosmetic`. Full report and the per-skill verdict table: [`research/2026-07-29-windows-portability-audit.md`](research/2026-07-29-windows-portability-audit.md). Spec: [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-windows-portability-audit-fixes.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-30-windows-portability-audit-fixes.md). This release ships the five that were safely verifiable without a Windows machine.

**1. `.gitattributes` — the whole plugin, not one skill.** The install path is a `git clone` (`~/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/mega-sdd/.git`), `core.autocrlf` is unset there so it inherits the machine's global config, and no `.gitattributes` existed anywhere. Git for Windows' *default* installer option is `core.autocrlf=true`. Measured effect: `set -u\r` → `set: -: invalid option`, `case "$1" in\r` → syntax error, and a heredoc terminator `PYEOF\r` never matching `<<'PYEOF'` — the plugin has **124 heredocs across 87 files**. Under real bash 5.3, `hooks/run-hook.sh` exits RC=2 dispatching nothing. The rule is `* text=auto eol=lf`, **not** `*.sh`: the eight hook entry points are extensionless. Two office laptops work today only because their global git happens to be `false`/`input` — luck, not design. **Note: this does not renormalize an existing clone; an affected machine must re-clone the plugin cache.**

**2. The unbounded `ast-grep` twin v5.10.0 missed.** `run-preflight-scan.sh:225` scanned the repo per v2 Hard rule, per unit, unbounded — while its byte-identical sibling in `_lib/postflight_rules.py` was bounded last release with the rationale written in this repo. The more useful half: `tests/hooks/bounded-subprocess.test.sh`, written specifically to prevent this class, globbed `_lib/*.py` only, so **12 unbounded calls in `.sh` heredocs stayed invisible and it reported green.** The matcher now covers `scripts/*.sh` heredocs and the extensionless files in `hooks/`. *A guard whose scope is narrower than the class it claims to cover reports green forever.*

**3. `generate-units` Step 7.5 had no spawn-cost gate.** It rebuilds a full-repo tree-sitter symbol graph — one process per FILE — while the prose documented "~5-10s". At the measured 220 ms/spawn that is **~37 min for a 10,000-file repo**. Gets the v5.11.0 gate treatment plus an `--auto` policy, so it is not a no-op in the autonomous lane where a stall strands someone with nobody watching. `N` now has one owner and a two-tier source: scan-codebase's persisted enumeration (exact, one spawn), falling back to `codebase-map.md` §2 as a FLOOR.

**4. `--shallow-scan` hashed one spawn per file.** Measured on 2,000 files: 2,000 spawns / 50.4 s per-file vs **1 spawn / 319 ms batched (158×)** — and it happened *before* the v5.11.0 gate's `N` even exists, so the gate could not see it. Under EDR a hash spawn costs the same ~220 ms as a tree-sitter spawn, so the unbatched "fast path" burned 2,000 spawns to avoid 1,990: **net-negative on Windows**, now stated as such. Also fixed: `-path './node_modules' -prune` anchors at the repo root, so a monorepo's `packages/*/node_modules/` was enumerated and hashed anyway (proven in a scratch repo).

**5. `timeout` — and two defects found underneath it.** The three prose sites gain `-k 2`. **Severity correction:** this is `degraded`, not `hang` — primary sources (`git-for-windows/msys2-runtime` PR#15, PR#16, commit `c967bd8`) show MSYS2 escalates SIGTERM to `TerminateProcess` after ~10 s, so the worst case is a bounded delay. `-k` is kept because it makes the ceiling explicit rather than implicit. Underneath: **stock macOS ships neither `timeout` nor `gtimeout`**, so the literal prefix returned rc 127 and the verdict table marked *every installed tool* `missing` — live since v5.9.0, now a resolved prefix that can never become a verdict about the probed tool. And seven matrix rows carry `tree-sitter --version || tree-sitter-cli --version`, where the shell parses `||` at the top level: only the first limb was bounded, the fallback ran unbounded, and `||` returned the *last* status, swallowing the 124/137. Wrapped as `$BOUND sh -c "<verify_cmd>"`.

Two review rounds ran over these patches; both found real defects the implementers had introduced, and two tests were strengthened past their brief because a reviewer proved they could be defeated — `xargs -0 -n1` (a two-character edit restoring the per-file fan-out) passed the batching test, and `test-pagerank-spawn-gate.sh` asserted a citation existed without checking it matched the cited file, letting a **fabricated claim** ship green. It now reads both ends. Invariant 5 is only enforced if something does.

**Not closed here:** `scripts-hardcode-python3-no-resolver` — 84 of 94 scripts hardcode `python3` and exactly one consumes `$MEGA_SDD_PY`; on today's laptops that means the validator lane exits 49, matches no halt clause, and the moat is dead while the model believes it is gated. That is the audit's worst finding and a larger change than an audit-response batch should carry. Every `NEEDS-WINDOWS` item remains unproven — no Windows machine was available, and §4 of the report collects them as one Git Bash script.

Suite: **178 pass / 0 fail** across both test trees.

## [5.11.0] - 2026-07-30

fix(scan-codebase): the tree-sitter engine spawns one process **per file** — on a Windows box with endpoint security that is the hang, and nothing warned.

Field report: `scan-codebase` hangs on the office laptop. The cause is a scale assumption calibrated on POSIX.

| engine | invocations |
|---|---|
| `tree-sitter` | **one per FILE** (`scan-procedure.md`: "one call per source file") |
| `regex` (ripgrep) | **one per LANGUAGE** ("one call per language") |

A ~1000× difference in spawn count for a typical repo. At the ~18 ms/spawn of a dev Mac it is invisible; at the **~220 ms measured on the team's laptops** it decides whether the scan finishes:

| files | tree-sitter, Windows |
|---:|---:|
| 200 | 44 s |
| 2,000 | **7.3 min** |
| 10,000 | 37 min |
| 100,000 | **6.1 hours** |

The only existing guard was `>100k files → confirm`, which at 220 ms/spawn is a **six-hour** threshold — it never fires in practice. And `--shallow-scan` does not help a *first* scan, since its invalidation gate reuses unchanged files by sha and on a first run every file is new. So an ordinary 2,000-file repo stalled with no warning and no obvious escape.

Adds a **mandatory spawn-cost gate before Step 5**: estimate `N × per_spawn` (0.22 s on `windows-bash`, 0.02 s elsewhere) and, above 60 s, ask before extracting — offering `--engine=regex` (seconds, lower precision), continue with tree-sitter, or narrow with `--include`. The engine is never downgraded silently: `precision_tier` is reported in the map, so the trade is the user's to make.

**Batching is the structural fix and is deliberately NOT shipped here.** Collapsing N spawns into one `tree-sitter query` call per language would remove the problem rather than manage it, but the batched capture-output format could not be verified — this machine's tree-sitter ships no compiled grammars (the exact "brew installs the CLI with zero grammars" case the procedure already documents). Shipping an unverified change to the extraction path is how the v5.8.0 regression happened. Recorded in `scan-procedure.md` with what is needed to verify it.

## [5.10.0] - 2026-07-30

fix(gates): the repo-wide `ast-grep` scan in the B1 gate path ran unbounded — inside the blocking PreToolUse hook.

Generalising the v5.9.0 regression, on the observation that the unbounded-child problem is not specific to install-deps. Surveying every `subprocess` call in `scripts/_lib/` (the libraries hook-invoked validators import) found three unbounded calls, one of them in the worst possible place:

- **`postflight_rules.py:540`** — `ast-grep scan --rule … <cwd>`, a **repo-wide** scan. Per the contract, B1 postflight is *recomputed at the execute-bolts gate*, so this executes inside the **blocking** PreToolUse hook. Unbounded work in a path Claude Code waits on is the exact shape of the 2026-07-28 Windows hang.
- **`dep_manifest.py`** — two `git show` / `git diff` calls, lower risk but same class.

The repo already had the right pattern — `state_probes.py` used `timeout=` — it simply was not applied consistently. That is the kind of drift a mechanical check catches and prose does not.

**The timeout fails CLOSED.** A Hard rule whose check did not complete has *not* been shown to hold, and this verdict feeds a blocking gate; treating "ran out of time" as `pass` would let a timeout launder a violation. A timeout now takes the same branch as a tool error: `verdict: fail`, with evidence naming the 120 s bound and how to narrow the rule.

New `tests/hooks/bounded-subprocess.test.sh` walks the AST of every file in `scripts/_lib/` and fails on any `subprocess.run`/`Popen`/`check_output` without `timeout=`. It refuses to report green if the matcher finds zero calls (a vacuous pass), carries a control fixture proving the matcher flags an unbounded call while ignoring a bounded one, and separately asserts the gate-path timeout yields `fail` rather than `pass`.

Scope note, stated honestly: an earlier count in this investigation claimed eight hook-invoked scripts run heavy external tools. That count was **mention-based and wrong** — `mmdc` and `pandoc` appear in those files only in comments. Verified by execution site, the actionable surface was three subprocess calls, one of which mattered.

## [5.9.0] - 2026-07-30

fix(install-deps): **v5.8.0 regression** — the new execution probes shipped with no timeout and no pre-filter, and stalled an audit on a corporate Windows laptop.

Field report: v5.7 completed on one office laptop; v5.8 hung on a colleague's. The cause is in v5.8.0's own fix. Converting 39 `verify_cmd` values from `command -v` to `<tool> --version` was right for correctness, but `command -v` is a shell **builtin** — zero forks, and it cannot hang. An execution probe can.

Measured, macOS warm (Windows + EDR is roughly an order of magnitude worse):

| probe | time |
|---|---:|
| `semgrep --version` | **3880 ms** |
| `mmdc --version` | 384 ms |
| `markdownlint-cli2 --version` | 366 ms |
| all 9 tools, exec probes | **~5.1 s** |
| all 9 tools, `command -v` (v5.7) | **53 ms** |

**~96× slower**, unbounded, and run for every tool including ones that are not installed. On a network where a proxy blackholes a tool's startup update check, that is not slow — it is stopped.

Three parts to the fix, all in the procedure rather than the matrix:

1. **`command -v` pre-filter first, mandatory.** Not on PATH → `missing` immediately, no exec probe. Only tools that are actually present pay the cost.
2. **`timeout 10 <verify_cmd>`** — never unbounded, in both Step 2 (audit) and Step 6 (post-install verify).
3. **Exit 124 → `present`/`verified` with a `slow-verify` note, never `missing`.** `command -v` already proved the binary exists; calling a slow probe a missing tool would propose a reinstall of something already installed.

Anti-hallucination rail 12 and three new assertions in `test-verify-cmd-usability.sh` pin all three, so this cannot silently return.

The v5.8.0 usability invariant is unchanged and still correct — presence genuinely cannot prove usability where an alias stub exists. What was wrong was making every tool pay an unbounded exec for it.

## [5.8.1] - 2026-07-30

fix(install-deps): `fix-windows-path.sh` reported "registry write failed" when it had never reached the registry.

Found while rehearsing the v5.8.0 acceptance script against a fake plugin cache. An interpreter without the `winreg` module — an MSYS2/Cygwin Python rather than a native Windows one — raised `WindowsPathError`, which the handler mapped to exit 5, documented as *"registry write failed or did not round-trip"*.

That is a misdiagnosis with a real cost: the operator would go looking for a failed or partial write, possibly try to restore from a backup, when in fact nothing was read or written and the actual remedy is a different Python. Now exit **7** / `not_native_python`, with the remedy named in the message. Propagated to the rc tables in `references/windows-path.md` and `SKILL.md` Step 6.

## [5.8.0] - 2026-07-30

fix(install-deps): four Windows defects from one field run — a stale PATH read as a failed install, `verify_cmd` testing presence instead of usability, winget's msstore source killing every install, and a remedy that cannot be followed where it is needed.

A team laptop (Windows 11, Git Bash, winget, PowerShell blocked by Group Policy, CrowdStrike) ran `/mega-sdd:install-deps`. First every install failed with exit 248/94; after a retry succeeded, **every install exited 0 and every `verify_cmd` still failed** — and the audit had reported `python3` as present on a machine with no Python at all.

**A — a stale PATH is not a failed install.** An installer writes `HKCU\Environment\Path`; a bash session already running never sees it (winget prints "restart your shell to use the new value"). A `verify_cmd` failing right after an install therefore carries no information. New `scripts/fix-windows-path.sh` + `scripts/_lib/windows_path.py` answer the question that does distinguish them — *will this resolve in a NEW shell?* — by composing the persisted system + user PATH from the registry. Step 6 now routes through it on `os = windows-bash`: resolvable → `verified` plus "restart the terminal", not a halt. Also repairs the directories Windows never adds to PATH at all (`pipx` → `~\.local\bin`, `pip --user` → `%APPDATA%\Roaming\Python\Python<XY>\Scripts`), gated behind a mandatory backup.

**B — `verify_cmd` tested presence, not usability.** 39 of the 46 `verify_cmd` values were literally `command -v <tool>`, so "run verify_cmd" and "test presence" were the same operation for every tool except python3. That is why the field output read `✓ jd (command -v jd — RC=0)`, and it is the mechanism behind the python3 false positive: a WindowsApps alias stub sits on the default PATH, resolves to `command -v`, prints "Python was not found…" to stderr and exits 49. v5.4.0 fixed this for the *hooks*; the *audit* was never updated. All 39 are now execution probes (`--version` measured rc 0 on all nine tools), and `command -v` may only ever produce `missing`, never `present` — it stays as a cheap fork-free pre-filter, which matters at ~220 ms/spawn on an EDR box. **Exit code is the verdict, never a stdout pattern**: `semgrep --version` prints an upgrade banner ahead of the version.

**C — winget's msstore source killed the whole command.** Verified in winget-cli source rather than inferred: `winget install` is the one search command that does *not* set `TreatSourceFailuresAsWarning`, so a failing source terminates the run even though it also prints the matches it found in the `winget` source. `0x8A15005E` is `PINNED_CERTIFICATE_MISMATCH` — corporate TLS inspection breaks winget's msstore cert pin **permanently**, so this recurs on that network forever; `0x801901F8` is HTTP 504. Exit 248/94 are POSIX low-byte truncations of those two HRESULTs — two failures, not four. `--source winget` is now pinned on all 7 winget routes. (The operator's workaround was right; their stated reason, "source ambiguity", was not — genuine ambiguity has its own code, `0x8A150016`, which never appeared.)

**D — the scoop remedy is unreachable where it is most needed.** The matrix calls `scoop install python` the only route yielding a working `python3`, and every fail-closed hook message steers there — but scoop's bootstrap is PowerShell-only (`irm get.scoop.sh | iex`). On a PowerShell-blocked image the sole documented remedy cannot be followed, and nothing said so. Now stated in both the matrix note and `os-detection.md`, with winget named as the fallback (verify `python`, not `python3`).

Anti-hallucination rails 8-11 encode the methods that must never be used, each observed to fail destructively on that machine: `reg add` from Git Bash (mangles backslashes, prints `ERROR: Invalid syntax` **while returning RC=0**), a hand-written `.reg` + `reg import` (**truncated a 798-char USER PATH to 92**), and `setx PATH` (1024-char cap, expands `%VAR%`, destroys `REG_EXPAND_SZ`).

Also recorded, and explicitly **not** a plugin defect: `EUNKNOWN: uv_spawn` on `powershell.exe`. mega-sdd invokes `bash` in all 9 hook entries and ships no `.ps1`; Claude Code enables its PowerShell tool automatically when it cannot find Git Bash. Host-side fix in `tooling-install.md`: `CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH` + `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_POWERSHELL_TOOL: "0"`. Generic `OTEL_*` keys cannot cause it.
